*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on 2026-08-13.*

It arose from that epic's work but is not required for it to complete: the queue
can be finished and correct without ever gaining capture/amend events. It also
has its own plan file, its own scope decisions and its own deferred phases — a
child with a plan file of its own is usually an epic wearing a child's clothes.
Left as a level-2 task rather than declared an epic, since it has no children
yet; it will become one naturally if capture and amend split.

[[file:~/.claude/plans/capture-amend-queue.md][Plan]]

*Plan approved 2026-08-13, scoped by two decisions the user made during
planning:* v1 covers *capture and amend* only — the structural writers
(=org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling=, =org_sort_children=)
stay immediate — and capture *writes through when the file is free*,
queueing only when it is busy, so today's immediacy survives and only
contended writes defer. That is the user's original lock idea made
non-blocking, and it supersedes the "always queue" reading of step 3
below.

Note the plan lives in its own file rather than this conversation's
=hazy-moseying-sky.md=: that path is already the permanent link for [[id:6b1e73c4-25da-4f0e-8a51-9c0d3f7ab214][6b1e73c4]] and
[[id:f290161c-f9e7-4bfb-9adf-4c5630d4c5ae][f290161c]], and Claude Code reuses one plan file per conversation, so planning
here overwrote it. Restored from =plans/= and the new plan given its own name,
so both older links still resolve to the plan they were written for.

*Read by the user 2026-08-13* — both the original draft below and the
=create-lockfiles= research appended to step 1. The "study this before
signing off" reminder it was captured under is discharged.

*No verdict was recorded, and none should be inferred.* The user said
only that they had reviewed it and that the heading could be amended
accordingly; they did not approve the recommendation, reject it, or
choose between its three steps. So this remains a proposal Claude
drafted, not an agreed design, and the three load-bearing claims are
still open to challenge: that a *blocking* lock is the wrong primitive,
that the underlying need is a missing queue rather than a missing lock,
and that the ordering below is the right one. Anyone acting on this
should get an explicit decision first.

*The ask, in the user's words:* so many times they want to ask a
question or make a proposal while something else is in flight, which
often leads to something new to capture or amend. The literal proposal
was a file or Emacs buffer lock/semaphore on =TODO.org= and friends,
causing concurrent sessions to wait their turn while another holds it.

*What actually collides today.*

The queue already removed most of the contention: =todo= and clock
events are append-only, per-session, written blind with =O_APPEND=, so
two sessions cannot corrupt each other there by construction. What
remains is *direct writes to the file*:

- =org_capture=, =org_refile=, =org_archive=, =org_move_sibling= and
  =org_sort_children=, which all still =save-buffer= immediately
- the org skill's prose edits via Claude Code's =Edit= tool, which
  bypass Emacs entirely and write to disk
- the user's own hand-edits in the live buffer
- =.git/index=, a separate incident already owned by
  [[id:2aeb65d6-350e-4c75-811e-4a748044fe2c][2aeb65d6]]

Not hypothetical: on 2026-08-13 two edits in one session hit "the file
had been modified on disk since you last read it". The ad-hoc
mitigation in use — checking =buffer-modified-p= before touching the
file — is a hand-rolled version of what this heading proposes.

*Why a blocking lock looks like the wrong primitive.*

Recorded as the argument against, because "why not just take a lock" is
the obvious question a future reader will ask.

1. *Waiting reintroduces the failure this architecture exists to avoid.*
   A session blocked on a lock burns wall-clock with no way to report
   why, and waits forever if the holder crashed. Same shape as the
   non-interactive =emacsclient= hang that pushed this project to
   append-only.
2. *The contention is asymmetric.* The human is the authoritative
   writer; Claude sessions should yield to them and never the reverse. A
   symmetric semaphore models the wrong relationship.
3. *It cannot be enforced where it matters most.* The =Edit= tool
   consults neither Emacs nor any lockfile, so any lock is advisory for
   exactly the writer most likely to be mid-flight. A mechanism that
   looks like mutual exclusion but is not is worse than an honest
   advisory one.
4. *Stale locks are stale clocks again.* An owner, a TTL and a recovery
   path — the subsystem
   [[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]] is currently
   deciding whether to retire, rebuilt for a new resource.

*The reframe this rests on — the part most worth challenging.*

The ask may not be mutual exclusion at all. Note where the user's own
sentence ends: the interruption *leads to something new to capture or
amend*. That is a missing queue, not a missing lock — state changes
already work that way; capture and amend do not.

If that reframe is wrong — if the real need is genuinely "stop the
other writer" rather than "do not lose my thought" — then the
recommendation below is wrong too, and a fail-fast lock alone is the
answer.

*Proposed, cheapest first.*

1. *Turn on =create-lockfiles=.* Measured 2026-08-13: it is =nil=, Doom's
   default, so Emacs's own mechanism is currently off entirely. One
   line. It will not stop the =Edit= tool, but it makes Emacs-mediated
   collisions visible rather than silent. Checked before proposing
   anything bespoke, per
   [[id:c084553c-0621-4a96-9fa1-f32850aeec6a][c084553c]]'s standing
   instruction.

   *Why Doom disables it, checked 2026-08-13 at the user's request.*
   =lisp/doom-emacs.el:462= sets it under a =Backup & autosave files=
   heading, sharing one comment with =make-backup-files=: "Don't
   generate backups or lockfiles. ... This is better suited to version
   control, and I don't want world-readable copies of potentially
   sensitive material floating around our filesystem." Older Doom
   (=core/core-editor.el=) worded it the same way.

   That rationale is about *backups*, and does not actually describe a
   lock file. Per the Emacs Lisp manual, a lock file is a symlink named
   =.#FILE= whose *target* is the string =user@host.pid:boot=; it holds
   no contents of the edited file at all. So the "copies of sensitive
   material" argument does not apply to the thing it is being used to
   justify — lockfiles were bundled in with backups under one heading
   and inherited a comment written for backups. A residual privacy point
   survives (the target leaks username, host and PID), but that is a
   much smaller claim than the one made.

   The real-world objection in the wider community is *tooling*, not
   privacy: =.#FILE= is a dangling symlink, and file watchers and build
   tools choke on it (e.g. =ember-cli= issue #6527, "=(setq
   create-lockfiles nil)= shouldn't be necessary for Emacs usage"). That
   risk is low for this repo — nothing here watches the tree — but it is
   the reason to check before flipping it globally rather than
   per-directory.

   *And one point actively in favor, which is why this step is listed at
   all:* on detecting a lock, Emacs calls =ask-user-about-lock=, which
   offers grab / ignore / abort. It *asks*; it does not wait. That is
   precisely the fail-fast shape argued for in step 2, already
   implemented upstream.
2. *A fail-fast advisory lock in the MCP write tools* — a refusal, not a
   wait: "another session is writing TODO.org; queued instead." Fast
   failure is debuggable and cannot deadlock.
3. *Extend the queue to capture/amend proposals.* The interjection
   becomes an event, nothing waits, and the existing human review pass
   applies it — which also makes it reviewable rather than immediate,
   the property already chosen deliberately for state changes.

Steps 2 and 3 overlap: if 3 lands, 2 may be unnecessary, since there
would be nothing left that needs to write immediately.


* TODO Concurrent sessions share one git index, and explicit staging protects nothing :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Dev
:ID:       2aeb65d6-350e-4c75-811e-4a748044fe2c
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 16:20]
:END:
:LOGBOOK:
- State "NEXT"       from              [2026-08-12 Wed 16:22] \\
  user is blocked on this: no second session until a mechanism exists
- State "TODO"       from "NEXT"       [2026-08-17 Mon 12:11] \\
  Auto-demoted: superseded by sibling [[id:95087d8f-e16d-45d3-bdea-9b179dc672b4][Lint for headings that are valid org but structurally unintended]] becoming NEXT.
:END:

*This one actually happened, deliberately, as a resilience test.* On
2026-08-12 two sessions ran against this same checkout. This session
wrote a heading to TODO.org, staged it with an explicit pathspec
(=git add -- TODO.org=), and went to commit. The commit failed with
=nothing to commit, working tree clean=: the other session had already
committed, and its pathspec-less =git commit= took the whole index,
sweeping this session's 69 staged lines into
=a8dc693 "Review-buffer interaction fixes ..."= — a commit whose
message describes unrelated work and names two unrelated heading
=:ID:=s. Content survived; attribution did not. =36410e2= is an empty
commit recording that, since =a8dc693= was already pushed and
rewriting a pushed commit under a live concurrent session is worse
than the mislabelling.

*The mechanism is the index, not the files.* Every convention this
project already has is about file contents — check for a modified
buffer, stage explicitly, never =git add -A=. None of them touch the
actual shared mutable state, which is =.git/index=: one per checkout,
not one per session. Worse, following the convention *increased* the
damage. Staging early loaded this session's work into the index
*before* the other session committed; a pathspec-less =git commit= then
picked it up automatically. Had this session not staged at all, its
work would have been left alone.

*Not covered by the existing worktree heading, and this is the load-
bearing part.*
[[id:2e09adb7-6e29-499c-a40e-98cd2b327cff][2e09adb7]] concludes that
worktrees handle the *code* side — "one checkout + branch per session,
no shared-file collisions" — and that only the org files need to stay a
single canonical copy in the main checkout. Read against this incident
those two halves collide: the file that got swept up *was* TODO.org,
which by that heading's own =:ID:=-uniqueness and clock-fragmentation
argument is exactly the file that *cannot* be partitioned into a
worktree. So a worktree session must still write and commit TODO.org
in the main checkout, through the main checkout's index, and hits this
identically. *Worktrees do not fix this scenario; they fix everything
around it.* That is new information for 2e09adb7, which assumed the
code side was the whole problem.

*Related but distinct:*
[[id:3cb3f955-f10a-47cd-84ab-e629d73ea59d][3cb3f955]] already asks
whether =git commit -- TODO.org= is "safe," but scoped to a *single*
session not sweeping up its own unrelated hunks in the same file. This
heading is the cross-session case: another session's *staged* work, in
any file. Same command, different failure. Worth answering together.

*One mitigation is already verified.* Tested 2026-08-12 in a scratch
repo: with =A.txt= staged, =git commit -- B.txt= commits B only, leaves
A staged, and does not commit A. So a pathspec-scoped commit is
genuinely safe for the *other* session's work. The catch is that it
constrains the wrong party — safety depends on the *committing* session
complying, and the session at risk has no way to enforce it.

*Caveat found while defining the hook below:* =git commit -- <paths>=
*fails on an untracked file* — =error: pathspec 'NEW.txt' did not match
any file(s) known to git=. A new file must be =git add=-ed first, and
only then can be committed by pathspec. So for brand-new content (a new
plan, a new script) the staging window this mitigation exists to avoid
is unavoidable, and exposure persists between the add and the commit.
The mitigation is complete only for already-tracked files — which is
where this incident happened, TODO.org being tracked.

*Options for something robust, none chosen:*
- *Convention only:* ban =git add= outright in favour of
  =git commit -- <paths>=, in CLAUDE.md and the org skill. Cheapest,
  verified to work, and binds only sessions that follow it. Given both
  sessions here are Claude Code reading the same CLAUDE.md, this is
  less hollow than it sounds — but it is guidance, not a mechanism, and
  the user asked for a mechanism.
- *A =pre-commit= hook* in =.githooks/=. Defined below — the only
  option here that is a mechanism rather than guidance, and cheaper
  than first assumed.
- *Route git writes through the event queue* of
  [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]]. That queue
  already carries =session_id=/=agent_id= and already exists to stop
  concurrent sessions from mutating shared state directly, with a human
  applying batches later. Commits are the same shape of problem. The
  heaviest option and the most consistent with where the project is
  already going.

*The =pre-commit= option, defined.* Measured 2026-08-12 in a scratch
repo with a hook that printed its own environment. =GIT_INDEX_FILE=
identifies which command form invoked the commit:

| Command                  | =GIT_INDEX_FILE=            | Sweeps a staged file? |
|--------------------------+-----------------------------+-----------------------|
| =git commit=             | =.git/index=                | yes                   |
| =git commit --amend=     | =.git/index=                | yes                   |
| =git commit -a=          | =.git/index.lock=           | yes                   |
| =git commit -- <paths>=  | =.git/next-index-<pid>.lock= | no                   |

The hook can read *both* views at once: what the commit will contain
(=git diff --cached=) and what the real index holds
(=GIT_INDEX_FILE=$(git rev-parse --git-dir)/index git diff --cached=).
In the pathspec case those differ exactly as they should — commit
=B.txt=, real index still =A.txt=. =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID= also
propagates into the hook, confirmed by printing it.

*Proposed rule:* refuse when the form is a whole-index one (basename of
=GIT_INDEX_FILE= is =index= or =index.lock=) *and* the real index is
non-empty; fire only when =CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID= is set. That is the
sweep case and nothing else. =git commit -- <paths>= always passes;
=git commit -a= against an empty index passes, since no one's work is at
risk; a human committing from a plain terminal is never affected. The
message should name the staged paths and print the pathspec form to use
instead.

*This retires the "needs ownership tracking" objection*, which the
first version of this heading asserted and which is wrong: the command
*form* is a sufficient discriminator and the safe form is
self-identifying. Per-session ownership of staged paths would only be
needed for a more permissive rule ("allow the sweep if everything
staged is mine"), not worth building first. If it ever is, the
precedent for where such state lives is
=claude-code-ide-org-queue-directory= — per-session runtime files,
deliberately outside the repo.

*Known limitations, all of them accepted rather than solved:*
- =--amend= is *indistinguishable* from a plain pathspec-less commit —
  both report =.git/index= — so it gets refused too, absent an escape
  hatch such as an =ALLOW_SWEEP= environment variable.
- =--no-verify= bypasses the hook entirely. This makes the mistake loud
  rather than silent; it does not make it impossible, and it still
  constrains the committing party rather than protecting the session at
  risk.
- Merge/rebase/cherry-pick paths are *untested* — the scratch-repo run
  errored before reaching them, and they need checking before shipping.
- Opt-in per clone via =core.hooksPath=, inheriting the same setup
  caveat =pre-push= already has.

*Testing* would follow the existing =bin/*-test= pattern
(=sync-plans-test=, =queue-append-test=): a scratch repo, each of the
four command forms, asserting exit status and message.

*Blocked on this in practice:* the user has stated they will not run a
second concurrent session until a mechanism is in place, so this gates
concurrent work generally rather than being one more backlog item.


* MAYBE Decide what this module may change on a user's machine when installed :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Dev
:ID:       1caed585-3209-4108-8501-eba16baf6a01
:CREATED:  [2026-08-12 Wed 11:54]
:END:

Raised by the user 2026-08-12, from a narrow question — how to set
=org-log-into-drawer= permanently — that turned out to have a general
answer: *a distributable package configures its own behavior, and does
not silently reconfigure the user's.* Worth deciding once, because the
module already crosses that line in more than one place and each
crossing is cheap to fix now and expensive to fix after other people
depend on it.

*The specific inventory, as of 2026-08-12:*

1. *Four global hooks, installed unconditionally at load*
   (=config.el:1869-1873=): one =org-blocker-hook= and three
   =org-trigger-hook= functions. Anyone who enables =:tools
   claude-code-ide-org= gets auto-clock-in on every transition to
   =DOING=/=PLANNING=, =NEXT= demotion, and sole-=TODO= promotion
   applied to *every org file they open* — not only this project's.
   These are opinionated GTD policies, and they are the bigger problem
   here, not the smaller one.
2. *A hardcoded assumption about one user's config*
   (=config.el:2702-2708=): the comment reads "With =org-log-into-drawer=
   nil (this user's setting)". The =let=-binding it justifies is
   correct and should stay — binding locally rather than globally is
   exactly the right instinct, and it is already documented as
   deliberate — but the *default* wants to be a =defcustom= rather than
   an assumption about whoever wrote it.

*The precedent already set, same day.* The =org-log-into-drawer=
question was resolved by adding =#+STARTUP: logdrawer= to this repo's
own =TODO.org=/=DONE.org= headers rather than by =setq=-ing the
variable. Verified: =#+STARTUP:= sets it *buffer-locally*, leaving the
global untouched, so the scope claimed matches the scope owned. That is
the shape the rest of this should follow.

*The module is not starting from zero here.* Eight =defcustom=s already
exist (=-queue-directory=, =-capture-file=, =-working-hours=,
=-query-files=, =-session-recovery-enabled=, =-audit-log-file=,
=-clock-status-file=, =-guidepost-gap-threshold=). Configuring its own
behavior is established practice; the gap is the global hooks and the
one hardcoded assumption, not the general habit.

*Genuinely open, and the reason this is =MAYBE= rather than =TODO=:*
whether a *Doom module* earns more latitude than a *MELPA package*. A
Doom module is opted into explicitly, per user, by editing =init.el= —
which is arguably consent to exactly these policies, and the hooks are
much of why someone would enable it at all. A MELPA package is a
library, and the same behavior on load would be indefensible. The two
targets may want different answers, or one guarded entry point
(=claude-code-ide-org-setup=) that installs nothing until called. Decide
that before doing the work, since it determines whether the hooks move
behind a toggle or stay as they are.

*Related:* [[id:ab75d6d2-0e59-405d-92c6-2c67488db133][ab75d6d2]] changes
one of these very hooks, so whichever lands second should not re-litigate
the other's decision.
[[id:02aaae22-0958-4c0f-b5bb-2cbea32416ae][02aaae22]] owns the file-header
convention the =#+STARTUP:= line was added to.


* TODO Read power assertions as a review-time attribution signal :code:research:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Queue
:ID:       1a5a5254-f62d-46e7-9a6c-3d84f2f52873
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 15:52]
:END:

CLAUDE.md records =pmset -g log= as "not yet attempted" and frames the
per-app power assertions as noise obscuring clean sleep/wake
transitions. For *this* question the framing is backwards: a laptop that
never sleeps emits zero transitions and a rich assertion record, and the
assertion holder is the answer.

Tried 2026-08-11 against a known-answer window. The log named the
competing activity exactly:

: 14:01:56  Created   NoDisplaySleepAssertion  (zoom.us, PID 58601)
: 14:42:14  Released  NoDisplaySleepAssertion  00:40:17

The user's own estimate was "roughly 14:00 to roughly 14:45" — the log
was right to the second, and the 40m17s sits entirely inside the 54m11s
permission-block of
[[id:f4e628ce-e653-429a-8f3c-0f5d4241b139][f4e628ce]].

*Not a validated method, on one observation.* Zoom happened to hold a
cleanly-named assertion; =coreaudiod= held 158 of the 361 entries in the
same window and is a far noisier proxy. An assertion also is not
inattention — people work through calls. The most it can honestly do is
prompt the human reviewer, never decide.

*Retention bounds it:* the log went back ~7 days (earliest 2026-08-04).
That puts a deadline on "the human applies at a moment of their own
choosing."

Revises the disposition recorded in
[[id:9d2fcdad-9bf7-47b6-8018-223b13ec4577][9d2fcdad]]'s neighbourhood,
which proposed closing the =pmset= lead as won't-do — correct for the
stale-interval guess heuristic, wrong for attribution.

*Promoted out of [[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] on
2026-08-14,* as a capability rather than a requirement. Note it is
explicitly *not* the =pmset= lead that
[[id:7771fc63-9374-4c8a-9e80-24ceec21977c][7771fc63]] closed as
won't-do: that one guessed when a stale clock stopped, this one assigns
an existing span to a heading at review time. Same data source,
different question, and only the first was refuted.

*A ground-truth absence window, reported by the user 2026-08-25 and worth keeping because this heading has none.* They started a =/next-session= run and left immediately, confirming at 17:59 that they had been away since roughly 16:56 and still were. So the interval =[2026-08-25 Tue 16:56]--[2026-08-25 Tue 17:59]+= is a *known* absence, stated rather than inferred -- which is exactly the kind of label any proposed signal has to be scored against, and exactly what the corpus lacks.

*Note what the session's own guideposts said about that hour, because it is the whole problem.* The agent ran continuously and productively throughout it -- two headings worked, a commit landed, four probe scripts ran -- so =Stop=/=UserPromptSubmit= bracketing produced one long unbroken span of genuine *agent* work. Nothing in the queue distinguishes that hour from an hour with a human watching every turn. Both look identical from the queue, and both are correctly credited as agent time; what is missing is orthogonal to the clock rather than an error in it.

*Which sharpens what a power-assertion signal would be for.* Not correcting a clock -- the clock was right. It would answer a different question the review pass asks constantly: whether a span had a human *available to be interrupted*, and therefore whether the work in it has had any human judgement applied to it at all. Everything this session queued is unreviewed in that stronger sense, and no existing field records the difference.

*Caveat on using this datum.* It is one window, reported to the minute by memory, with a soft start ("immediately after kicking off" -- the session's first event was 16:56) and no end yet. Good enough to sanity-check a signal's shape; not a benchmark.

*Tested 2026-08-25 against a known event, and the result corrects this heading's own proposal.* The user reported that the Mac slept mid-session because the clamshell was closed, and could not say for how long -- so there was ground truth for the *fact* and none for the *bounds*, which is exactly the case this heading is for.

*The signal is real, but it is not the one proposed here.* This heading asks for *power assertions*. Those are precisely the noise =7771fc63= dismissed: =pmset -g log= for the session window is dominated by =PID 5048(Claude) Summary NoIdleSleepAssertion "Electron"= every fifteen minutes, =AddressBookSourceSync=, =storagekitd=, =coreaudiod= -- hundreds of lines, no transition. What answered the question was the =Notification= domain in the same log, two lines:

: 2026-08-25 17:06:53  Notification  Display is turned off
: 2026-08-25 17:50:00  Notification  Display is turned on

*It agrees with the queue to the second.* =clock_in= on [[id:82df2a6c-881d-4117-a752-5b3b04c9656d][82df2a6c]] was queued at 17:06:54, one second after the display went off; the =resume= guidepost is at 17:50:14, fourteen seconds after it came on. Neither number was available to the other mechanism.

*Cross-validated against a third, independent source* rather than trusted on the coincidence: mtimes of the probe scripts this session wrote to its scratch directory show execution at 17:07:07, 17:07:35, 17:08:10, 17:08:55, 17:09:39, 17:09:45 -- and then nothing until 18:02. So work continued for about three minutes after the lid closed and stopped somewhere in 17:09:45--17:50:00.

*The crucial limitation, and it is what stops this being a drop-in fix.* =Display is turned off= means *the lid closed*, not *the machine stopped*. The same session has a counterexample two hours later: display off 18:05:47, on 18:10:34, with continuous work throughout ([[id:f421c5c3-f81f-4ebf-99c9-42c53cc00558][f421c5c3]]'s probes wrote at 18:06:08, 18:10:09 and 18:11:33). Something held an assertion that time and the machine kept running.

*So the two edges are not equally useful, which is the finding worth keeping:*

- *The wake edge is a reliable *resume* signal.* Display-on means someone opened the lid, and work can restart from there. Cheap and unambiguous.
- *The sleep edge is *not* a reliable stop signal.* It bounds when work *may* have stopped and must be combined with evidence of absence -- no queue events, no file writes -- to say that it did.

*What it would have bought today.* [[id:82df2a6c-881d-4117-a752-5b3b04c9656d][82df2a6c]] was credited 0:03 for roughly seven minutes of real work. The wake edge anchors the post-wake segment exactly; the pre-sleep segment is bounded below by its own =clock_in= and above by the last artefact, giving 17:06:54--17:09:45. Neither bound is a guess. The sleep *onset* stays unbounded within a forty-minute window, and should stay that way in any record.

*Note the shape of the correction.* This is the second time the useful signal turned out to be a different line in a log that had already been dismissed wholesale. Worth re-reading a rejected source for the *specific* record wanted, rather than re-litigating the rejection.


* TODO Make task lifecycle answerable from one timeline, starting at creation :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Queue
:ID:       d9132c57-9dcf-47e9-ba74-7f5a1ced3ae5
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 10:32]
:END:

Prompted 2026-08-11 by a concrete reporting question — "how long did it
take from capture time to the PLANNING phase, for each of these tasks?"
— which is the first properly articulated demand on this project's
second stated goal, trustworthy tracking of where attention actually
went. It cannot be answered today, and the reason is not the one it
looks like.

*Measured 2026-08-11:*

: TODO.org  headings carrying :CREATED:   56
: TODO.org  "- State ..." lines            7
: DONE.org  "- State ..." lines            0

All seven come from the two hooks that write their note explicitly
(=--trigger-auto-promote-sole-todo=, =--promote-planning-to-doing=). Not
one came from =org_set_todo=, which binds =org-inhibit-logging= to =t=
unconditionally for every state. So the transition history barely
exists, and in DONE.org — where post-hoc analysis would actually look —
it does not exist at all. That zero is *not* the archiver stripping
drawers: =org-archive-subtree= moves the whole subtree intact. Nothing
was ever written.

The example question is the worst case of this. =PLANNING= is transient
— auto-promoted to =DOING= the instant a plan is approved — and the
same census found *zero* headings sitting in it. Current state can
therefore never answer "when did this reach PLANNING"; only a
transition log can.

*So the blocker is the missing log, not =:CREATED:='s location,* and
[[id:b5f7c5c7-7ad6-4c68-9cce-3479db1f1644][b5f7c5c7]] already fixes it:
apply runs inside a genuinely interactive command, which is what lets
org's native logging fire instead of being suppressed. This heading is
the remaining half — making the timeline start at the right place — and
is close to worthless before the cutover, since there would be a
creation line and then silence.

*Proposal: duplicate =:CREATED:= into =:LOGBOOK:= as a creation entry.
Do not move it.* As a property it is cheaply queryable — org-ql filters
and sorts on properties directly — and drawer text is not. Moving it
trades an easy query for a parse. Duplicating buys one timeline and one
parser: analysis becomes "read the entries in order" rather than "read
a property, read a drawer, reconcile two timestamp conventions." It also
gives [[id:af7d3687-6d1e-4480-a9e2-df772785713f][af7d3687]]'s single
chronological drawer somewhere to begin.

*org already has the shape, so nothing needs inventing.* With no
previous state org renders the field as an empty string — =org.el= line
11092, =(or org-log-note-previous-state "")= — producing a native
creation entry with no previous state.

*Corrected 2026-08-11, twice, both by direct test rather than reading.*

1. *The literal above was wrong.* The empty string is padded, not
   quoted, so the real rendering carries no ="= pair at all:

   : - State "NEXT"       from              [2026-08-11 Tue 15:46]

   A parser keyed on =from ""= matches nothing. The claim that "existing
   parsers, and org-ql, handle it unmodified" was never verified and
   should be re-checked against that actual string before anything is
   built on it.

2. *=TODO= alone carried no =!= marker*, so the one transition this
   feature most needs — into =TODO=, the default keyword a capture
   receives — logged nothing, silently and without error. Interactivity
   is necessary for native logging but not sufficient; logging also has
   to be *requested*. Fixed by
   [[id:90a5a0cf-a81d-45dc-a453-0bfe61e3ef93][90a5a0cf]]. Had this
   shipped as written, it would have produced no creation entries for
   precisely the common case and looked like a bug in apply.

*The alternative, which may well be better and is not yet weighed:*
synthesise the creation entry *at read time*, in whatever reporting
function consumes this, from the =:CREATED:= property that already
exists. Nothing in any file changes, there is no duplication to diverge,
and no migration. The cost is that every consumer must know to do it,
where writing it once puts it in the record for anything that reads the
drawer — including a human. Decide this before writing anything.

*If the write-it-down option wins, two consequences:*
- *Backfill.* 56 headings have =:CREATED:= and no creation entry. A
  one-time pass can synthesise them from the property — mechanical and
  safe, with precedent in the retrospective consolidation pass recorded
  in DONE.org. Without it the analysis has a cutoff date.
- *It depends on [[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]].*
  =org_capture= does not write =:CREATED:= at all, so headings born from
  the tool have no creation time for a backfill to read. Fix that first
  or those headings have no origin in either place.

*Mechanism, if written at creation time:* it need not be special. Under
the queue model creation can simply *be* the =capture= event proposed in
[[id:7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f][7fa68d5c]], with apply
writing its =:LOGBOOK:= entry exactly as it writes a state transition.
The two headings are one feature approached from different sides —
capture durability there, timeline origin here.

*One semantic caveat for whoever writes the parser:* "created" and
"acquired its first TODO keyword" are not the same event in general.
They coincide throughout this project because every task heading is born
with a keyword, but the equivalence is a local accident, not a rule, and
a heading that gains a keyword later would make them diverge.


* TODO Fall back to the queue when org_capture cannot reach Emacs :code:
:PROPERTIES:
:CATEGORY: Queue
:ID:       7fa68d5c-b6c0-43df-85c6-764b1d56511f
:CREATED:  [2026-08-11 Tue 07:31]
:END:

=org_capture= requires a live Emacs. When the server is unreachable the
call fails outright and the task exists only in the conversation, which
ends. Not hypothetical: on 2026-08-10 the Emacs server was down for the
first ten minutes or so of a session (=emacsclient= reporting "can't
find socket"), and any capture attempted then would simply have been
lost.

This is the one creation path that cannot use the property the event
queue exists for — durable, cheap, no Emacs required. Everything else a
session records survives an Emacs outage; the act of writing down a new
task does not.

*Proposal:* attempt the live capture; on failure, append a =capture=
event to the queue, and let apply create the heading.

*Three things this raises, none of them settled:*
- *It is the queue's first outbox use.* Every existing kind is a
  proposal about a heading that already exists; a =capture= event would
  bring one into being. That is a genuinely different contract, and the
  review buffer would need to render it as such.
- *The :ID: must be minted by the session, not at apply time.* An ID
  assigned on application is useless to the session that queued it — it
  could not link to the heading, block on it, or clock against it, which
  is exactly what =claude-code-ide-org-capture= returns its ID for
  today. Minting without Emacs is fine (=org-id-new= is a UUID), but it
  means an ID can be referenced before the heading it names exists.
- *Reconciliation.* If the live capture succeeded and only its reply was
  lost, applying the queued event would duplicate the heading. A
  session-minted ID gives apply a cheap idempotency key: if that :ID:
  already resolves, the event is already realised.

*The larger caveat, which bears on this and on
[[id:cdcc065c-628d-4350-b1ba-fcf686e9c4c1][cdcc065c]] both.* This tool creates a bare
=* TODO <title>= at top level in a single target file. It cannot place a
heading under a named parent, nor carry tags, a body, or any property
beyond the =:ID:= it generates. Consequently almost every heading in
this project is written by direct Elisp instead — four were created that
way on 2026-08-10 alone, none through the tool. So instrumenting or
repairing =org_capture= improves a path that is rarely taken, and the
prior question is whether the tool should become capable enough to be
worth using. Decide that first; it may reorder or absorb both headings.

*Considered and set aside:* queueing a =capture= event on *every*
capture, as an audit record carrying =session_id=/=agent_id= — which is
the only place session attribution exists, and would answer "which
session created this heading?" structurally rather than from prose in
the body. Rejected for now on the caveat above: it would record almost
nothing real. Revisit if the tool becomes the normal creation path.


*Also the timeline origin:* recording creation as a =:LOGBOOK:= entry
([[id:d9132c57-9dcf-47e9-ba74-7f5a1ced3ae5][d9132c57]]) can ride this same event rather
than needing a mechanism of its own — apply would write the creation
entry exactly as it writes a state transition.
